\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{8}}{Left: Example pVPA $\Delta$ with the following return and diverge probabilities:
$[cZ{\downarrow}c]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{6}}$,
$[cZ{\downarrow}r]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{12}}$,
$[rZ{\downarrow}r]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}$,
$[rZ{\downarrow}c]=\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}$,
and $[c{\uparrow}]=\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}$,
$[\tau{\uparrow}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}$,
$[r{\uparrow}]=0$.
In general, these probabilities may be irrational numbers~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:journals/jacm/EtessamiY09}{}{}]}.
Right: The step chain $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}$ according to \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{def:stepMC}.
The transition probabilities can be computed using the return and diverge probabilities and \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:transProbs}.
}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[. ]{{Figure 8}}{Left: Example pVPA $\Delta$ with the following return and diverge probabilities:
$[cZ{\downarrow}c]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{6}}$,
$[cZ{\downarrow}r]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{12}}$,
$[rZ{\downarrow}r]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}$,
$[rZ{\downarrow}c]=\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}$,
and $[c{\uparrow}]=\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}$,
$[\tau{\uparrow}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}$,
$[r{\uparrow}]=0$.
In general, these probabilities may be irrational numbers~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:journals/jacm/EtessamiY09}{}{}]}.
Right: The step chain $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}$ according to \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{def:stepMC}.
The transition probabilities can be computed using the return and diverge probabilities and \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:transProbs}.
}}}
\@add@centering\end{figure}\par Recall from \eqref{eq:markov_prop} that the stochastic process $V^{(0)},V^{(1)}\ldots$, where $V^{(i)}\in Q\cup Q\bot$ is the extended state at the $i$th step, has the Markov property.
Since $Q\cup Q\bot$ is a finite set, we can now use \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:transProbs} to construct the underlying finite Markov chain explicitly.
\par\begin{definition}$\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}$ is the Markov chain with states
$$M\hskip 3.5pt=\hskip 3.5pt\{\,q\in Q_{\mathsf{call}}\cup Q_{\mathsf{int}}\,\mid\,[q{\uparrow}]>0\,\}~\cup~Q\bot~,$$initial state $q_{0}\bot$, and for all $v,v^{\prime}\in M$, the probability of transition $v\to v^{\prime}$ is defined according to \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:transProbs}.
\hfill$\triangle$\end{definition}\par\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:stepMcExampleAdvanced} depicts a non-trivial pVPA and its step chain.
In this example, all return and diverge probabilities are rational.
In general, however, the return and diverge probabilities (\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{def:returnDiverge}) are algebraic numbers that are not always rational or even expressible by radicals~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:journals/jacm/EtessamiY09}{}{}]} (cf.\ \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{ex:nonRationalProbs}).
As a consequence, one cannot easily perform numerical computations on the step chain.
However, the probabilities can be encoded implicitly as the unique solution of an \emph{existential theory of the reals} (ETR) formula, i.e., an existentially quantified FO-formula over $(\mathbb{R},+,\cdot,\leq)$~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{esparzaMCPPDA-lics}{}{}]}.
Since the ETR is decidable in $\mathsf{PSPACE}$, many questions about the step chain are in $\mathsf{PSPACE}$ as well.
We will make use of this in \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:resStPaDVPA} below.
\par The property of $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}$ that is most relevant to us is given by the following \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:stepChainSound}.
For $\rho\in Runs_{\Delta}$, we let $\rho{\Downarrow}_{\mathit{Steps}}=V^{(0)}(\rho)V^{(1)}(\rho)\ldots\in(Q\cup Q\bot)^{\omega}$ (note that this is a slightly different ``footprint'' than the one introduced in \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{sec:stair-parity}).
\par\restatable[Soundness of $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}$]{lemma}{stepChainSound}
Let $\Delta$ be a pVPA with step chain $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}$.
Let $M$ be the states of the step chain and let $R\subseteq M^{\omega}$ be measurable.
Then
$$\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}(\{\,\rho\in Runs_{\Delta}\,\mid\,\rho{\Downarrow}_{\mathit{Steps}}\in R\,\})\hskip 3.5pt=\hskip 3.5pt\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}}(R)$$where $\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}$ and $\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}}$ are the probability measures associated with $\Delta$ and $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}$, respectively.
\begin{proof}The formal proof requires some basic notions from measure theory.
In fact, \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:stepChainSound} is actually an instance of the following more general statement:
\par\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@[($\star$)]\emph{Let $(X,\mathcal{A},\mu)$ and $(Y,\mathcal{B},\nu)$ be probability spaces such that $\mathcal{B}=\sigma(\mathcal{G})$, where $\mathcal{G}\subseteq 2^{Y}$, i.e., $\mathcal{B}$ is the $\sigma$-algebra generated by the sets in $\mathcal{G}$.
Assume furthermore that $\mathcal{G}$ is a $\pi$-system, i.e., $\mathcal{G}$ is non-empty and closed under finite intersections.
Let $f\colon X\to Y$ be such that for all $G\in\mathcal{G}$, $f^{-1}(G)\in\mathcal{A}$ and $\mu(f^{-1}(G))=\nu(G)$.
Then $f$ is a measurable function and the pushforward measure $\nu^{\prime}=\mu\circ f^{-1}$ coincides with $\nu$.}
\end{enumerate}\par\par We now explain how to prove ($\star$) using fundamental measure theory.
The fact that $f$ is measurable follows because the inverse image $f^{-1}$ preserves set operations (see, e.g., \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{ash2000probability}{}{}, below~Definition~1.5.1]}).
For the claim that $\nu^{\prime}=\nu$ it suffices to note that by assumption we have for all $G\in\mathcal{G}$ that $\nu^{\prime}(G)=\nu(G)$, and since $\mathcal{G}$ is a $\pi$-system, an application of the $\pi$-$\lambda$ theorem (see, e.g.,~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{panangaden2009labelled}{}{}, Proposition~2.10]}) implies that $\nu^{\prime}=\nu$.
\par We instantiate ($\star$) as follows to prove \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:stepChainSound}:
The probability spaces are the ones associated with the measures $\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}$ and $\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}}$.
In particular, the $\sigma$-algebra on which $\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}}$ is defined is the one generated by the cylinder sets $\mathcal{C}=\{w.M^{\omega}\mid w\in M^{*}\}\subseteq 2^{M^{\omega}}$.
It is easy to see that $\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{C}\cup\{\emptyset\}$ is a $\pi$-system, and $\sigma(\mathcal{C})=\sigma(\mathcal{G})$.
We define $f\colon(Q\times\Gamma_{\text{-}\bot}^{*}.\bot)^{\omega}\to M^{\omega},f(\rho)=\rho{\Downarrow}_{\mathit{Steps}}$, i.e., $f$ projects a run from $\Delta$ to its footprint of steps (which is a run in $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}$).
To apply $(\star)$ it remains to show that for all cylinder sets $w.M^{\omega}$, $w\in M^{*}$, we have that
(i) $f^{-1}(w.M^{\omega})$ is measurable, and
(ii) $\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}(f^{-1}(w.M^{\omega}))=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}}(w.M^{\omega})$.
For (i) notice that $f^{-1}(w.M^{\omega})=\bigwedge_{i=0}^{|w|-1}(V^{(i)}=w(i))$ is indeed measurable because it is a finite intersection of measurable events by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:VwellDef}; recall that $V^{(i)}$ denotes the (extended) state at the $i$th step.
(ii) is trivial in the case where $|w|=0$, so we let $|w|=n+1$, $n\geq 0$, and exploit the properties of the step chain $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}$.
If $w(0)\neq q_{0}\bot$ (the initial state of $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}$) then $\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}(V^{(0)}=w(0))=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}}(w.M^{\omega})=0$.
Otherwise $w(0)=q_{0}\bot$.
In this case, if $n=0$ (i.e., $|w|=1$), then $\mathbb{P}_{\Delta}(V^{(0)}=w(0))=\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}}(w.M^{\omega})=1$.
Else, if $n>0$ ($|w|>1$), by the Markov property from equation \eqref{eq:markov_prop}, we have
\@@amsalign
where $P$ is the transition probability function in the Markov chain $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}$ and the last equality holds by definition of the probability measure $\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{M}_{\Delta}}$.
\end{proof}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Main Result of \texorpdfstring{\Cref{sec:modelCheckingDVPA}}{Section 4}}
The following is the main result of \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{sec:modelCheckingDVPA}:
\par\restatable{theorem}{restateMainResDet}
Let $\Delta$ be a pVPA and $\mathcal{D}$ be a stair-parity DVPA, both over the same pushdown alphabet $\Sigma$.
Then for all $\theta\in[0,1]\cap\mathbb{Q}$, the following is decidable in $\mathsf{PSPACE}$:
$$\mathbb{P}(\{\rho\in Runs_{\Delta}\mid\lambda(\rho)\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{D})\})\geq_{?}\theta~.$$\par The rest of \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{sec:proofMainRes} is devoted to the proof of \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:resStPaDVPA}.
We first provide a brief overview.
The first step is to construct the product $\Delta\times\mathcal{D}$ according to \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{def:prod}.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:product} we need to compute the stair-parity acceptance probability of $\Delta\times\mathcal{D}$.
\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:stepChainSound} reduces this to computing a usual parity acceptance probability in the step chain $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}$.
This can be achieved through finding the bottom strongly connected components (BSCC) of $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}$, classifying them as \emph{good} (the minimum priority of a BSCC state is even) or otherwise as \emph{bad}, and running a standard reachability analysis w.r.t.\ the good BSCCs (see \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:productAndStepChain} for an example).
The remaining technical difficulty is that the transition probabilities of $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}$ are not rational in general.
We handle this using the fact that these probabilities are expressible in the ETR~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{esparzaMCPPDA-lics}{}{}]}.
\par\par\par We now present the formal proof.
We use the following result about return probabilities of pPDA, which is originally due to~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{esparzaMCPPDA-lics}{}{}]}:
\begin{lemma}The return probabilities $[pZ{\downarrow}q]$ are expressible in ETR.
More specifically, there exists an FO-formula $\Phi$ over $(\mathbb{R},+,\cdot,\leq)$ which uses just existential quantifiers and free variables $\langle pZ{\downarrow}q\rangle_{p,q\in Q,Z\in\Gamma}$ such that $\Phi$ becomes a true FO-sentence iff each free variable $\langle pZ{\downarrow}q\rangle$ is substituted by the actual return probability $[pZ{\downarrow}q]$.
Moreover, $\Phi$ can be effectively constructed in polynomial space.
\end{lemma}\par\begin{lemma}The next-step probabilities (i.e., the transition probabilities of the step chain) in \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:transProbs} are expressible in ETR.
\end{lemma}\begin{proof}With \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:retProbsFormula} it suffices to note that ETR expressible numbers are closed under addition, multiplication and division.
Let $x,y\in\mathbb{R}$ be expressed by ETR formulae $\Phi(x)$ and $\Phi^{\prime}(y)$, respectively.
Then the formula $\Phi^{\prime\prime}(z)\coloneqq\exists x,y\colon\Phi(x)\land\Phi^{\prime}(y)\land z=x+y$ where $z$ is a fresh variable expresses the sum of $x$ and $y$, and similar for multiplication.
For division, we have that $\Phi^{\prime\prime}(z)\coloneqq\exists x,y\colon\Phi(x)\land\Phi^{\prime}(y)\land z\cdot y=x$ expresses $x/y$ provided that $y\neq 0$ (if $y=0$ then $\Phi^{\prime\prime}$ does not express a unique real number as it is then either unsatisfiable or trivial).
\end{proof}\par\begin{table}\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{3}}{
The underlying graph of the step chain.
The condition in each cell is true iff the corresponding transition probability in \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:transProbs} is non-zero.
}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[. ]{{Table 3}}{
The underlying graph of the step chain.
The condition in each cell is true iff the corresponding transition probability in \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:transProbs} is non-zero.
}}}
\centering{\par\noindent\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\ignorespaces\begin{tabular}[]{l c c c c }\hline\cr\hline\cr&$q\to r$&$q\bot\to r$&$q\bot\to r\bot$&$q\to r\bot$\\
\hline\cr$q\in Q_{\mathsf{call}}~$&\noindent\hbox{}\hfill{{\hbox{\begin{tabular}[c]{@{}c@{}}$[r{\uparrow}]>0~\land~($\\
$(\exists r^{\prime},Z\colon P_{\mathsf{call}}(q,r^{\prime}Z)>0\land[r^{\prime}Z{\downarrow}r])>0)$\\
$~\lor~\exists Z\colon P_{\mathsf{call}}(q,rZ)>0)$\end{tabular}}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}\hfill{{\hbox{\begin{tabular}[c]{@{}c@{}}$\exists Z\colon P_{\mathsf{call}}(q,rZ)>0$\\
$~\land~[r{\uparrow}]>0$\end{tabular}}}}\hfill\hbox{}&\noindent\hbox{}\hfill{{\hbox{\begin{tabular}[c]{@{}c@{}}$\exists r^{\prime},Z\colon P_{\mathsf{call}}(q,rZ)>0$\\
$~\land~[r^{\prime}Z{\downarrow}r]>0$\end{tabular}}}}\hfill\hbox{}&false\\
\hline\cr$q\in Q_{\mathsf{int}}$&$[r{\uparrow}]>0~\land~P_{\mathsf{int}}(q,r)>0$&false&$P_{\mathsf{int}}(q,r)>0$&false\\
\hline\cr$q\in Q_{\mathsf{ret}}$&false&false&$P_{\mathsf{ret}}(q\bot,r)>0$&false\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\end{tabular}
}}}}}
}
\@add@centering\end{table}\par We now describe our $\mathsf{PSPACE}$ algorithm to prove \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:resStPaDVPA}.
\par{Step 1.}
We first construct the product pVPA $\tilde{\Delta}=\Delta\times\mathcal{D}$ with priority function $\Omega^{\prime}\colon Q\to\mathbb{N}_{0}$ where $Q$ are the states of $\tilde{\Delta}$ as in \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{def:prod}.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:product} it suffices to compute the probability
\@@amsalign
that the footprint of a run in the product $\tilde{\Delta}$ satisfies the parity condition induced by $\Omega^{\prime}$.
$\tilde{\Delta}$ can be constructed in polynomial time.
\par{Step 2.}
We express \eqref{eq:probInProduct} using the step chain $\mathcal{M}_{\tilde{\Delta}}$.
Let $M\subseteq Q\cup Q\bot$ be the states of the step chain $\mathcal{M}_{\tilde{\Delta}}$.
Let $\Omega^{\prime\prime}\colon M\to\mathbb{N}_{0}$ be the extension of $\Omega^{\prime}$ to the states of $M$ via $\Omega^{\prime\prime}(q)=\Omega^{\prime\prime}(q\bot)=\Omega^{\prime}(q)$ for all $q\in Q$.
That is, $\Omega^{\prime\prime}$ induces a parity acceptance set $\mathit{Parity}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}\subseteq M^{\omega}$ which is $\omega$-regular and thus measurable.
Let $\rho\in Runs_{\tilde{\Delta}}$.
Clearly, $\rho{\downarrow}_{\mathit{Steps}}\in\mathit{Parity}_{\Omega^{\prime}}$ iff $\rho{\Downarrow}_{\mathit{Steps}}\in\mathit{Parity}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}$.
Thus by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:stepChainSound}, \eqref{eq:probInProduct} is equal to
$$\mathbb{P}(\{\,\pi\in Runs_{\tilde{\Delta}}\,\mid\,\rho{\downarrow}_{\mathit{Steps}}\in\mathit{Parity}_{\Omega^{\prime}}\,\})\hskip 3.5pt=\hskip 3.5pt\mathbb{P}(\mathit{Parity}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime}})$$where the right hand side is the probability that a run of the step chain $\mathcal{M}_{\tilde{\Delta}}$ satisfies the parity condition induced by $\Omega^{\prime\prime}$.
We have thus reduced the problem of computing a stair-parity acceptance probability in the product pPDA $\tilde{\Delta}$ to computing a standard parity acceptance probability in the finite Markov chain $\mathcal{M}_{\tilde{\Delta}}$.
\par The rest of the proof uses standard techniques and is similar to the proof of~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{esparzaMCPPDA-lics}{}{}, Theorem 5.15]}.
The main technical difficulty is that the transition probabilities of $\mathcal{M}_{\tilde{\Delta}}$ cannot be written in an explicit numerical form since they are in general algebraic numbers.
\par{Step 3.}
We construct the \emph{underlying graph} $G_{\tilde{\Delta}}$ of the step chain $\mathcal{M}_{\tilde{\Delta}}$, i.e., we determine the set $M$ of states and include a directed edge between states $v,v^{\prime}\in M$ iff the probability of transition $v\to v^{\prime}$ is positive.
\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:constructG} gives sufficient and necessary conditions for this in all cases (\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:constructG} can be seen as the ``qualitative'' version of \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:transProbs}).
The conditions in \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:constructG} (as well as constructing the state space of $\mathcal{M}_{\tilde{\Delta}}$) require checking if $[q{\uparrow}]>0$ for states $q\in Q$ of $\tilde{\Delta}$.
The latter is equivalent to checking if $\sum_{r\in Q}[qZ{\downarrow}r]<1$, which is reducible to ETR by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:retProbsFormula} and hence decidable in $\mathsf{PSPACE}$.
\par{Step 4.}
We determine the bottom strongly connected components (BSCC) of $G_{\tilde{\Delta}}$ from the previous step by a standard (efficient) graph analysis.
We mark the BSCCs $B\subseteq M$ such that $\min_{v\in B}\Omega^{\prime\prime}(v)$ is even as ``good'', the others as ``bad''.
It is well-known that in the finite Markov chain $\mathcal{M}_{\tilde{\Delta}}$ it holds that
$$\mathbb{P}(\rho\in\mathit{Parity}_{\Omega^{\prime\prime}}\mid\rho\text{ reaches a good BSCC})=1$$due to the Long-Run Theorem of finite Markov chains: Each state of a BSCC is visited infinitely often almost-surely, provided that this BSCC is reached at all.
Moreover, if a run $\rho$ reaches a bad BSCC, than the probability to satisfy the parity condition is zero and thus
\@@amsalign
Thus it only remains to compute the probability to reach a good BSCC in $\mathcal{M}_{\tilde{\Delta}}$.
\par{Step 5.}
We use the previous step to classify the states $M$ of the step chain $\mathcal{M}_{\tilde{\Delta}}$ into three categories: $M_{=0}$ contains all states from which no good BSCC is reachable in the graph $G_{\tilde{\Delta}}$, $M_{=1}$ contains all good BSCCs, and $M_{?}$ contains all other states.
We recall that the probabilities to reach $M_{=1}$ are the \emph{unique} solution of the following linear equation system (see, e.g., \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{MCbible}{}{}, Ch.\ 10]}):
\@@amsalign
We can treat the vectors of probabilities $\vec{p}$ and the variables $\vec{x}$ in this equation system as free variables of an ETR formula $\mathcal{R}(\vec{p},\vec{x})$.
By \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:transProbETR}, there is an ETR formula $\Phi(\vec{p})$ expressing $\vec{p}$.
The ETR formula
$$\exists\vec{p},\vec{x}\colon\hskip 3.5pt\Phi(\vec{p})\hskip 3.5pt\land\hskip 3.5pt\mathcal{R}(\vec{p},\vec{x})\hskip 3.5pt\land\hskip 3.5ptx_{v_{0}}\geq\theta$$is thus true iff the probability to reach a good BSCC from initial state $v_{0}\in M$ is at least $\theta$.
Truth of this formula can be decided in $\mathsf{PSPACE}$, which concludes the proof of \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:resStPaDVPA}.
\qed\par\begin{figure}\centering\par\noindent\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\ignorespaces\hbox to159.71pt{\vbox to133.66pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 42.6727pt\lower-120.65355pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

\@add@centering\end{figure}\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Implications for Probabilistic One-counter Automata}
\par A probabilistic visibly \emph{one-counter automaton} (pVOC) is the special case of a pVPA with unary stack alphabet, i.e., $|\Gamma_{\text{-}\bot}|=1$.
For example, the pVPA in \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:stepMcExampleAdvanced} (left) is a pVOC.
For many problems, better complexity bounds are known for pVOC than for the general case.
In particular, $[p{\uparrow}]>_{?}0$, i.e., the question whether a pVOC started in state $p$ never reaches counter value (or stack height) zero with positive probability, can be decided in $\mathsf{P}$~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{brazdilSurvey}{}{}, Theorem~4]}.
We can exploit this to improve \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:resStPaDVPA} in the pVOC case:
\par\begin{corollary}Let $\Delta$ be a pVOC and $\mathcal{D}$ be a stair-parity DVPA over pushdown alphabet $\Sigma$.
The problem $\mathbb{P}(\{\rho\in Runs_{\Delta}\mid\lambda(\rho)\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{D})\})=_{?}1$ is decidable in $\mathsf{P}$.
\end{corollary}\begin{proof}The key observation is that, since we can efficiently decide $[p{\uparrow}]>_{?}0$, we can efficiently (in polynomial time) construct the underlying graph $G_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}$ of the step chain of $\Delta\times\mathcal{D}$ (as in the proof of \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:resStPaDVPA}), and then apply polynomial-time graph analysis algorithms to check if only good BSCCs are reachable in $G_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}$.
\end{proof}\par\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:resStPaDVPAqual} implies that there exist efficient algorithms for many properties of pVOC-expressible random walks on $\mathbb{N}_{0}$.
In fact, almost-sure satisfaction of each \emph{fixed} visibly-pushdown property can be decided in $\mathsf{P}$.
For instance, using the DVPA from \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:stepsExample} we can decide if a random walk is repeatedly bounded with probability $1$.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{Model Checking against Büchi VPA and CaRet}
\par With \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:StPaDVPA} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{thm:resStPaDVPA} it follows immediately that quantitative model checking of pVPA against non-deterministic Büchi VPA is decidable in $\mathsf{EXPSPACE}$.
We can improve the complexity in the qualitative case:
\restatable{theorem}{thmresNVPAQual}
Let $\Delta$ be a pVPA and $\mathcal{A}$ be a (non-deterministic) Büchi VPA over the same pushdown alphabet.
The problem $\mathbb{P}(\{\rho\in Runs_{\Delta}\mid\lambda(\rho)\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A})\})=_{?}1$ is $\mathsf{EXPTIME}$-complete.
\begin{proof}The lower bound is due to \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:conf/tacas/EtessamiY05}{}{}, Theorem~8]} and already holds for non-pushdown Büchi automata.
We now describe an $\mathsf{EXPTIME}$ decision procedure:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@We first determinize $\mathcal{A}$ using \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:StPaDVPA} which is possible in time exponential in $|\mathcal{A}|$.
Let $\mathcal{D}$ be the resulting stair-parity DVPA and consider the product $\Delta\times\mathcal{D}$ (\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{def:prod}).
Note that the product can be constructed in polynomial time in $|\mathcal{D}|$ and $|\Delta|$, and thus in exponential time in the overall size of the input.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@The crucial observation for the next step is that $[q{\uparrow}]=[(q,s){\uparrow}]$ for all states $(q,s)$ of $\Delta\times\mathcal{D}$.
This holds because by definition of the product, $\mathcal{D}$ merely \emph{observes} the runs of $\Delta$, and thus the diverge probabilities of $\Delta\times\mathcal{D}$ and $\Delta$ are essentially the same.
We compute the set $Div_{\Delta}=\{q\mid[q{\uparrow}]>0\}\subseteq Q$, where $Q$ are the states of $\Delta$, in \emph{exponential time in $|\Delta|$} using a $\mathsf{PSPACE}$ decision procedure for the ETR~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{esparzaMCPPDA-lics}{}{}]}.
Note that computing $Div_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}=\{(q,s)\mid[(q,s){\uparrow}]>0\}$ directly would take \emph{doubly-exponential} time in $|\mathcal{A}|$; the proposed ``optimization'' is thus essential for obtaining the $\mathsf{EXPTIME}$ upper bound.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@We now determine the set of triples $Ret_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}=\{(q,Z,p)\mid[qZ{\downarrow}p]>0\}$ in $\Delta\times\mathcal{D}$.
Unlike the diverge probabilities, this set can be computed in \emph{polynomial} time in the size of $\Delta\times\mathcal{D}$ (hence exponential in the size of the input) because we may disregard the exact transition probabilities and conduct a standard reachability analysis in a non-deterministic pushdown automaton~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:reference/mc/AlurBE18}{}{}]}, also see \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{brazdilSurvey}{}{}, p.~136]}.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@The next step is to construct the underlying \emph{graph} $G_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}$ of the step chain $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}$, i.e., the directed graph that has the same vertices as $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}$ and includes an edge $(u,v)$ iff the 1-step transition probability from $u$ to $v$ is positive in the Markov chain.
This can be done in polynomial time in $|\Delta\times\mathcal{D}|$ using the sets $Div_{\Delta}$ and $Ret_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}$ defined above and \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{fig:constructG}.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@The final step is, as in \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:resStPaDVPA}, to determine the BSCCs of $G_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}$, classify them as good or bad according to whether they satisfy the (standard) parity condition inherited from $\mathcal{D}$, and then check if there is a bad BSCC reachable from the initial state.
All these steps can be done in polynomial time in $|\Delta\times\mathcal{D}|$.
\end{itemize}\end{proof}\par In the above result, membership in $\mathsf{EXPTIME}$ relies on the fact that one can construct the underlying \emph{graph} of a step chain $\mathcal{M}_{\Delta\times\mathcal{D}}$ in time exponential in the size of $\Delta$ but \emph{polynomial} in the size of $\mathcal{D}$.
$\mathsf{EXPTIME}$-hardness follows from~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:conf/tacas/EtessamiY05}{}{}, Theorem~8]}.
In fact, qualitative model checking of pPDA against \emph{non-pushdown} Büchi automata is also $\mathsf{EXPTIME}$-complete~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:conf/tacas/EtessamiY05}{}{}]}.
With \lx@cref{creftypepluralcap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:caretToNBVPA}, \lx@cref{refnum}{thm:StPaDVPA}, \lx@cref{refnum}{thm:resStPaDVPA} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{thm:resNVPAQual} we immediately obtain the following complexity results for CaRet model checking:
\begin{theorem}The quantitative and qualitative probabilistic CaRet model checking problems (\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{def:caretDecisionProblems}) are decidable in $\mathsf{2EXPSPACE}$ and $\mathsf{2EXPTIME}$, respectively.
\end{theorem}Both problems are known to be $\mathsf{EXPTIME}$-hard~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:conf/qest/YannakakisE05}{}{}]}.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{Conclusion}
\par We have presented the first decidability result for model checking probabilistic pushdown automata---an operational model of recursive discrete probabilistic programs---against CaRet, or more generally, against the class of $\omega$-VPL.
We heavily rely on the determinization procedure from~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{vpGames}{}{}, Theorem~1]} and the notion of a step chain used in previous works~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{esparzaMCPPDA-lics, esparzaMCPPDA}{}{}]}.
These two constructions turn out to be a natural match.
\par We conjecture that the upper bounds from \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:resCaRet} are not tight due to the exponential blow up incurred by applying the VPA determinization from~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{vpGames}{}{}, Theorem~1]}.
Future work is thus to investigate whether the doubly-exponential complexity can be lowered to singly-exponential, e.g., by generalizing the automata-less algorithm from~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:conf/qest/YannakakisE05}{}{}]}.
Another open question is whether existing results~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:journals/jacm/EtessamiY09,DBLP:journals/siamcomp/EsparzaKL10,DBLP:journals/jacm/StewartEY15}{}{}]} for \emph{approximately} computing the probabilities $[qZ{\downarrow}r]$ can be used for approximate quantitative CaRet and $\omega$-VPL model checking.
We also plan to extend our recent work on \emph{certificates}~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:conf/tacas/WinklerK23,DBLP:conf/lics/WinklerK23}{}{}]} to temporal and other logical properties.
Such certificates can be approximate as well.
\par Other future work includes exploring to what extent algorithms for probabilistic CTL can be generalized to the branching-time variant of CaReT~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:conf/spin/GutsfeldMN18}{}{}]},
considering more expressive logics such as visibly LTL~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:journals/jar/BozzelliS18}{}{}]} or OPTL~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:journals/tcs/ChiariMP20}{}{}]},
and studying the interplay of \emph{conditioning} and recursion~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{DBLP:conf/starai/StuhlmullerG12}{}{}]} through the lens of pPDA.
